% which of course boils down to \cstr{h = b} when each HeatBlock has exactly one heat generator (but this is not assumed to happen);
 %
 \item optionally, the \dimensions and variables necessary to deserialize a \code{DCNetworkData} object that describes the transmission network; see \S \ref{sec:DCNetworkData} for details. If that is not provided (basically, \cstr{NumberNodes} is not provided or it is \code{== 1}), then the transmission network is taken to have only one node (a bus). If the data of a \code{DCNetworkData} is specified, the \code{DCNetworkData} is passed to each of the \code{NetworkBlock} (see below) of the \code{UCBlock}, if any. However, if a \code{NetworkBlock} also has a \code{DCNetworkData} specified in its own group, then the \code{DCNetworkData} inside the \code{NetworkBlock} overrules that inside the \code{UCBlock}, which is ignored by that \code{NetworkBlock};
 %
 \item The \variable \cstr{ActivePowerDemand}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble}, and indexed both over the dimensions \cstr{NumberNodes} and \cstr{TimeHorizon}. This variable is optional if
 \begin{itemize}
  \item a \code{NetworkBlock} is defined for each time instant (see below), and
  \item each of the defined \code{NetworkBlock} has the \cstr{ActiveDemand} variable specified in the corresponding group.
 \end{itemize}
 Otherwise it is mandatory. When it is defined, the entry \code{ActivePowerDemand[ n , t ]} is  assumed to contain the active power demand of node $n$ of the transmission network at the given time instant $t$, where the first dimension \cstr{NumberNodes} can be read via \code{NetworkBlock::NetworkData::get\_number\_nodes()} from either the \code{DCNetworkData} object in \code{UCBlock}, or these in the \code{NetworkBlock}. When \cstr{ActivePowerDemand} is defined, and also the \cstr{ActiveDemand} variable is defined in the group of some \code{NetworkBlock}, then \cstr{ActiveDemand} overrules the value in the corresponding row of \cstr{ActivePowerDemand}, which is ignored.
 %
 \item the \groups \cstr{NetworkBlock\_0}, \cstr{NetworkBlock\_1}, \dots, \cstr{NetworkBlock\_t} with \code{t = \code{TimeHorizon} - 1}, containing each the constraints on the transmission network at time $t$. The \code{NetworkBlocks} are optional, but if any of them are missing, then
 \begin{itemize}
  \item \cstr{ActivePowerDemand} (see above) is mandatory in \code{UCBlock}.
  \item also the \code{DCNetworkData} (see above) is mandatory in \code{UCBlock}, unless the transmission network is a bus (that is, \cstr{NumberNodes} is not provided or it is \code{== 1}).
 \end{itemize}
 In particular, when a \code{NetworkBlock} is not defined for a given time instant t then one of these happen:
 \begin{itemize}
  \item If \code{NumberNodes == 1} (or it is not provided) then no \code{NetworkBlock} is constructed for that time instant, and the entry \code{ActivePowerDemand[ 0 , t ]} contains the active demand for $t$.
  \item If \code{NumberNodes > 1}, then a \code{DCNetworkBlock} is automatically constructed for that time instant, it is provided with the \code{DCNetworkData} object (which must be present in \code{UCBlock}) and the row \code{ActivePowerDemand[ ... , t ]} contains the active demand of each node at time instant $t$.
 \end{itemize}
 %
 \item the \variable \cstr{GeneratorNode}, of type \code{netCDF::NcUint} and indexed over the set {\{0, \dots , \code{NumberElectricalGenerators} - 1\}}; \code{GeneratorNode[ g ]} tells to which node of the transmission network, the specified electrical generator $g$ belongs. Note that this means that different electrical generators in the same \code{UnitBlock} can belong to different nodes of the transmission network. This is justified e.g. by hydro cascade units where different turbines can be rather far apart geographically, but still linked by (long) stretches of rivers. If \code{NumberElectricalGenerators == NumberUnits } (all \code{UnitBlock} have exactly one electrical generator), then this variable is indexed over \code{NumberUnits}. If \code{NumberNodes == 1} (say, it is not provided at all), then this variable need not be defined, since it is not loaded.
 %
 %\item the \variable \cstr{HeatNode}, of type \code{netCDF::NcUint} and indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberHeatBlocks}; the entry \code{HeatNode[ h ]} tells to which node of the transmission network \highlight{all} the heat generators that are also electrical generators in the \code{HeatBlock} $h$ belong. Note that this implies that, unlike electrical generators in a \code{UnitBlock}, heat generators in a \code{HeatBlock} are always ``geographically near to each other'' so that they are necessarily attached to the same node of the transmission network (which is a reasonable assumption since heat, unlike say water, usually cannot travel much far). If \code{NumberHeatBlocks == 0} (say, it is not provided at all), then this variable need not be defined, since it is not loaded. This information is actually only used to determine in which Pollutant Zone a \code{HeatBlock} is located, in order to add the corresponding heat generators (that are not also electricity generators) to the pollution constraints. This means that also if \code{NumberPollutants == } (say, it is not provided at all) this variable is useless and therefore need not be defined, since it is not loaded. Finally, notice that for heat generators into a \code{HeatBlock} that also are electrical generators, this variable provides again an information that is already known, i.e., to which node they belong to (cf. \cstr{GeneratorNode} above). Of course \highlight{the two information must agree}, otherwise the input file is ill-defined and exception is thrown.
 %
 %\item the \variable \cstr{HeatSet}, of type \code{netCDF::NcUint} and indexed over the set {\{0, \dots, \code{NumberHeatGenerators} - 1\}}. If \code{HeatSet[ h ] = k < NumberElectricalGenerators}, then $k$ is the unique name of the electrical generator corresponding to the heat generator $h$; otherwise the heat generator $h$ is not also an electrical generator. If \code{NumberHeatBlocks == 0} (there is no \code{HeatBlock}) then this variable need not be defined, since it is not loaded. It is possible that different heat generators correspond to the same electrical generator (that is, \code{HeatSet[ h1 ] == HeatSet[ h2 ]} for some \code{h1 != h2}), but not vice-versa: a heat generator either corresponds to a specific electrical generator, or to no electrical generator (it is an heat-only generator);
 %
 %\item the \variable \cstr{PowerHeatRho}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberUnits}: entry \code{PowerHeatRho[ i ]} is assumed to contain the electrical-power-to-heat ratio for \highlight{all generators} within unit $i$ (most likely, a single generator). This is only used in the constraints linking electrical units to heat units, hence if \code{NumberHeatBlocks == 0} (there are no \code{HeatBlock}) then this variable need not be defined, since it is not loaded;
 %
 \item the \dimension \cstr{NumberPrimaryZones} tells how many \cstr{primary spinning reserve zones} are there in the problem. The \dimension is optional, if it is not provided then it is taken to be $0$, which means that no primary reserve constraints are present in the problem;
 %
 \item the \variable \cstr{PrimaryZones}, of type \code{netCDF::NcUint} and indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberNodes}. The entry \code{PrimaryZones[ i ]} tells to which primary zone the node $i$ belongs: if \code{PrimaryZones[ i ] >= NumberPrimaryZones}, this means that node $i$ does not belong to any primary zone, and hence the corresponding electrical generators are not involved into the primary reserve constraints. If \code{NumberPrimaryZones == 0} (say, it is not provided at all) then this variable need not be defined, since it is not loaded. If \code{NumberPrimaryZones == 1} and this variable is not defined, then there is only one primary zone and all the nodes belong to it;
 %
 \item the \variable \cstr{PrimaryDemand}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and indexed both over the \dimensions \cstr{NumberPrimaryZones} and \cstr{TimeHorizon}: entry \code{PrimaryDemand[ i , t ]} is assumed to contain the primary reserves requirement which are specified on the primary reserve zone $i$ in the time t. If \code{NumberPrimaryZones == 0} (say, it is not provided at all), then this variable need not be defined, since it is not loaded;
 %
 \item the \dimension \cstr{NumberSecondaryZones} tells how many \cstr{secondary spinning reserve zones} are there in the problem. The \dimension is optional, if it is not provided then it is taken to be $0$, which means that no secondary reserve constraints are present in the problem;
 %
 \item the \variable \cstr{SecondaryZones}, of type \code{netCDF::NcUint} and indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberNodes}. The entry \code{SecondaryZones[ i ]} tells to which secondary zone the node $i$ belongs: if \code{SecondaryZones[ i ] >= NumberSecondaryZones}, this means that node $i$ does not belong to any secondary zone, and hence the corresponding electrical generators are not involved into the secondary reserve constraints. If \code{NumberSecondaryZones == 0} (say, it is not provided at all) then this variable need not be defined, since it is not loaded. If \code{NumberSecondaryZones == 1} and this variable is not defined, then there is only one secondary zone and all the nodes belong to it;
 %
 \item the \variable \cstr{SecondaryDemand}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and indexed both over the \dimensions \cstr{NumberSecondaryZones} and \cstr{TimeHorizon}: entry \code{SecondaryDemand[ i , t ]} is assumed to contain the secondary reserves requirement which are specified on the secondary reserve zone $i$ in the time t. If \code{NumberSecondaryZones == 0} (say, it is not provided at all), then this variable need not be defined, since it is not loaded;
 %
 \ \item the \dimension \cstr{NumberInertiaZones} tells how many \cstr{inertia constraints zones} are there in the problem. The \dimension is optional, if it is not provided then it is taken to be $0$, which means that no inertia constraints are present in the problem;
 %
 \item the \variable \cstr{InertiaZones}, of type \code{netCDF::NcUint} and indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberNodes}. The entry \code{InertiaZones[ n ]} tells to which inertia zone the node $n$ belongs: if \code{InertiaZones[ n ] >= NumberInertiaZones}, this means that node $n$ does not belong to any inertia zone, and hence the corresponding units are not involved into the inertia reserve constraints. If \code{NumberInertiaZones == 0} (say, it is not provided at all) then this variable need not be defined, since it is not loaded. If \code{NumberInertiaZones == 1} and this variable is not defined, then there is only one inertia zone and all the nodes belong to it;
%
 \item the \variable \cstr{InertiaDemand}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and indexed both over the \dimensions \cstr{NumberInertiaZones} and \cstr{TimeHorizon}: entry \code{InertiaDemand[ i , t ]} is assumed to contain the inertia reserves requirement which are specified on the inertia reserve zone $i$ in the time t. If \code{NumberInertiaZones == 0} (say, it is not provided at all), then this variable need not be defined, since it is not loaded;
%
 \item the \dimension \cstr{NumberPollutants} containing the number of pollutants in the problem. The \dimension is optional, if it is not provided then it is taken to be $0$, which means that no pollutants constraints are present in the problem;
 %
 \item the \variable \cstr{NumberPollutantZones} of type \code{netCDF::NcUint} and indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberPollutants}: the entry \code{NumberPollutantZones[ p ]} is assumed to contain the number of pollutant zones associated with pollutant $p$. If \code{NumberPollutants == 0} (say, it is not provided) then this variable need not be defined, since it is not loaded. Then, all number of pollutant zones associated with each pollutant $p$ is computed as: \code{TotalNumberPollutantZones == NumberPollutantZone[ 0 ] + \dots + NumberPollutantZone[ NumberPollutants - 1 ]};
%
 \item the \variable \cstr{PollutantZones}, of type \code{netCDF::NcUint} and indexed over the \dimensions \cstr{NumberPollutants} and \cstr{NumberNodes}: the entry \code{PollutantZones[ p , n ]} tells to which pollutant zone associated with pollutant $p$ the node $n$ belongs. If \code{PollutantZones[ p , n ] >= NumberPollutantZones[ p ]}, this means that node $n$ does not belong to any pollutant zone, and hence the corresponding units are not involved into the pollutant budget constraints associated with pollutant $p$. If \code{NumberPollutants == 0} (say, it is not provided) then this variable need not be defined, since it is not loaded;
 %
 \item the \variable \cstr{PollutantBudget}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and indexed over the set \{ 0 , \dots, \code{TotalNumberPollutantZones} - 1 \}: the entry \code{PollutantBudget[ n ]} for \code{n = 0 , \dots, TotalNumberPollutantZones - 1} is assumed to contain the pollutant budget (across all the time horizon) for the pair (zone of the pollutant , pollutant) corresponding to $n$. In another word, since the number of pollutant zones of each pollutant may not be equal with each other it is useful (to avoid having to store PollutantBudget as an irregular matrix) to be able to assign a unique index \code{n = 0, 1, \dots, TotalNumberPollutantZones - 1} to each pollutant budget of each pollutant zone. A mapping must be defined between each entry $n$ and the pair (zone of the pollutant , pollutant). The mapping is the obvious one: \code{UCBlock} has a set of pollutants \code{p = 0, 1, \dots, NumberPollutants - 1}, and each pollutant $p$ may have several pollutant zones (see comments of variable \cstr{NumberPollutantZones} above). Thus, in general the mapping is:
 \begin{itemize}
  \item \code{n = 0} corresponds to the zone $0$ of pollutant $0$
  \item \code{n = 1} corresponds to the zone $1$ of pollutant $0$
  \item \code{\dots}
  \item \code{n = NumberPollutantZone[ 0 ] - 1} corresponds to the zone \code{NumberPollutantZone[ 0 ] - 1} of pollutant $0$
  \item \code{n = NumberPollutantZone[ 0 ] } corresponds to the zone $0$ of pollutant $1$
  \item \code{n = NumberPollutantZone[ 0 ] + 1} corresponds to the zone $1$ of pollutant $1$
  \item \code{\dots}
 \end{itemize}
 \smallskip
 \noindent
 If \code{NumberPollutants == 0} (say, it is not provided) then this variable need not be defined, since it is not loaded;
 %
 \item the \variable \cstr{PollutantRho}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and indexed over three \dimensions which are \cstr{TimeHorizon} and \cstr{NumberPollutants} and the set \{0, \dots, \code{NumberElectricalGenerators} - 1\} (see comments above). The first \dimension can have size either $1$ or \cstr{TimeHorizon}. In the former case the entry \code{PollutantRho[ 0 , p , g ]} is assumed to contain the conversion factor of pollutant $p$ due to the electrical generator $g$ which is equal for all time instants $t$. Otherwise, the first \dimension has full size \code{TimeHorizon} and the entry \code{PollutantRho[ t , p , g ]} gives the conversion factor of pollutant $p$ due to the electrical generator $g$ for time t. If \code{NumberPollutants == 0} (it is not provided) then this variable need not be defined, since it's not loaded.
 %
 %\item the \variable \cstr{PollutantHeatRho}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and indexed over three \dimensions. The first \dimension can have size $1$ or \cstr{TimeHorizon}. The second and third \dimensions have sizes \cstr{NumberPollutants} and \cstr{NumberHeatBlocks}, respectively. If the first \dimension has size $1$, then the entry \code{PollutantRho[ 0 , p , h ]} is assumed to contain the conversion factor of pollutant $p$ due to the generation of \highlight{all} heat generators in \code{HeatBlock} $h$, which is equal for all time instants $t$. If, instead, the first \dimension has full size \cstr{TimeHorizon}, then the entry \code{PollutantRho[ t , p , h ]} gives the conversion factor of pollutant $p$ due to the generation of \highlight{all} heat generators in \code{HeatBlock} $h$ for time instant $t$. If \code{NumberPollutants == 0} (say, it is not provided) then this variable does not need be defined, since it is not loaded. If \code{NumberHeatBlocks == 0} (there is no \code{HeatBlock}) then this variable need not be defined, since it is not loaded.
\end{itemize}

%- - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - -
\subsection {\code{UnitBlock}}

The \code{UnitBlock} class, which derives from the \Block, defines a base class for any possible \cstr{unit} that can be attached to a \code{UCBlock}. A unit is in general a set of electrical generators tied together by some technical constraints, although many units actually correspond to only one generator. The base \code{UnitBlock} class only has very basic information that can characterize almost any different kind of unit:
%
\begin{itemize}
 \item The time horizon of the problem;
 %
 \item The number of generators in the unit ($1$ by default, see \code{get\_number\_generators()});
 %
 \item Four \code{boost::multi\_array< ColVariable, 2 >} objects, that are used to store the information regarding:
 %
     \begin{itemize}
      \item the commitment of the generators in the unit;
      %
      \item the primary spinning reserve of the generators in the unit;
      %
      \item the secondary spinning reserve of the generators in the unit;
      %
      \item the active power produced by the generators in the unit;

     \end{itemize}

\end{itemize}
%
Each of the \code{boost::multi\_array< ColVariable, 2 >} has as first \dimension the time horizon and as second \dimension the number of generators (as returned \code{get\_number\_generators()}). There are two possible cases:
\begin{itemize}
 \item if some \code{boost::multi\_array< ColVariable, 2 >} is empty(), then the corresponding variable does not exist (for instance, the generator in the unit may not have reserve);
 %
 \item otherwise, the \code{boost::multi\_array< ColVariable, 2 >}, say $M$, must have \code{f\_time\_horizon} rows and \code{get\_number\_generators()} columns: each element $M[ t , g ]$ gives the variable for time step $t$ of generator $g$.
\end{itemize}
%
The class also outputs some general information regarding how the active power and/or commitment status of each generator of the unit at a given time instant impact the unit's capability of satisfying inertia constraints, and the fixed consumption (if any) of each generator in the  unit when it is off.
\smallskip
\noindent
Besides the mandatory \cstr{type} attribute of any \dBlock, the \group representing the \code{UnitBlock} should contain the following:
%
\begin{itemize}
 \item the \dimension \cstr{TimeHorizon} containing the time horizon. The \dimension is optional because the same information may be passed via the method \code{set\_time\_horizon()}, or directly retrieved from the father if it is a \code{UCBlock}; see the comments to \code{set\_time\_horizon()} for details.
 %
 \item the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals} that is provided to allow that all time-dependent data in the \code{UnitBlock} can only change at a subset of the time instants of the time interval, being therefore piecewise-constant (possibly, constant). \cstr{NumberIntervals} should therefore be \code{<=} \cstr{TimeHorizon}, with four distinct cases:
 %
 \begin{enumerate}
 \item $1 < \cstr{NumberIntervals} < \cstr{TimeHorizon}$, which means that
   at some time instants, \textbf{but not all of them}, the values of some
   of the relevant data are changing; the intervals are then described in
   \variable \cstr{ChangeIntervals}.

 \item \label{NumberIntervals_eq_1} $\cstr{NumberIntervals} == 1$, which means that the value of each
   relevant data in the \code{UnitBlock} is the same for each time instant $0,
   \dots, \cstr{TimeHorizon} - 1$ in the time horizon. In this case, the
   \variable \cstr{ChangeIntervals} (see below) is ignored.

 \item \label{NumberIntervals_eq_TimeHorizon} $\cstr{NumberIntervals} ==
   \cstr{TimeHorizon}$, which means that values of the relevant data changes
   at every time interval (in principle; of course there is nothing preventing
   the same value to be repeated in the \netCDF input). Also in this case the
   \variable \cstr{ChangeIntervals} is ignored, since it is useless.

 \item The dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, which means that
   the values of the relevant data may be the same for each time instant (as
   in case \ref{NumberIntervals_eq_1} above) or indexed over
   \cstr{TimeHorizon} (as in case \ref{NumberIntervals_eq_TimeHorizon}
   above). Also in this case, of course, \cstr{ChangeIntervals} (see below) is
   ignored, and therefore it can (and should) not be present.
 \end{enumerate}
 %
 Note that this (together with \cstr{NumberIntervals}, if defined) obviously sets the \cstr{maximum frequency} at which data can change; if some data changes less frequently (say, it is constant), then the same value will have to be repeated. Individual data can also have specific provisions for the case where the data is all equal despite \cstr{NumberIntervals} saying differently.
 %
 \item the \variable \cstr{ChangeIntervals}, of type integer and indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals}. The time horizon is subdivided into \code{NumberIntervals = k} of the form $[ 0 , i_1 ]$,$[ i_1 + 1 , i_2 ]$, \dots $[ i_{k-1} + 1, \code{TimeHorizon} - 1 ]$; \cstr{ChangeIntervals} then has to contain $[ i_1 , i_2 , \dots i_{k-1} ]$ . Note that, therefore, \cstr{ChangeIntervals} has one significant value less than \cstr{NumberIntervals}, which means that \code{ChangeIntervals[ NumberIntervals - 1 ]} is ignored. Anyway, the whole variable is ignored if either \cstr{NumberIntervals} \code{<= 1} (such as if it is not defined), or \cstr{NumberIntervals} \code{>=} \cstr{TimeHorizon}.
\end{itemize}

\subsubsection {\code{ThermalUnitBlock}}

The \code{ThermalUnitBlock} class derives from \code{UnitBlock} and implements a \cstr{reasonably standard} thermal unit of a Unit Commitment Problem. That is, the class is designed in order to give mathematical formulation to describe the operation of large set of conventional power plants (such as nuclear, hard coal, gas turbine, gas, combined cycle, oil, \dots) which are directly connected to the transmission grid.
Besides the mandatory \cstr{type} attribute of any \dBlock, the group must contain all the data required by the base \code{UnitBlock}, as described in the comments to \code{UnitBlock::deserialize}(\code{netCDF::NcGroup}). In particular, we refer to that description for the crucial \dimensions \cstr{TimeHorizon}, \cstr{NumberIntervals} and \cstr{ChangeIntervals}. The \code{netCDF::NcGroup} must then also contain:
%
\begin{itemize}
 \item  the \variable \cstr{MinPower}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and either of size $1$ or indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals} (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then this \variable can also be indexed over \cstr{TimeHorizon}). This is meant to represent the vector \code{MnP[ t ]} that, for each time instant $t$, contains the minimum active power output value of the unit for the corresponding time step. If \cstr{MinPower} has length $1$ then \code{MnP[ t ]} contains the same value for all $t$. Otherwise, \code{MinPower[ i ]} is the fixed value of \code{MnP[ t ]} for all $t$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}. Note that it must be \code{MnP[ t ] >= 0} for all $t$. If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon}, then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{ChangeIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded;
 %
 \item  the \variable \cstr{MaxPower}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and either of size $1$ or indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals} (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then this \variable can also be indexed over \cstr{TimeHorizon}). This is meant to represent the vector \code{MxP[ t ]} that, for each time instant $t$, contains the maximum active power output value of the unit for the corresponding time step. If \cstr{MaxPower} has length $1$ then \code{MxP[ t ]} contains the same value for all $t$. Otherwise, \code{MaxPower[ i ]} is the fixed value of \code{MxP[ t ]} for all $t$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}. Note that it must be \code{MxP[ t ] >= 0} for all $t$. If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon}, then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{ChangeIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded;
%
 \item  the \variable \cstr{DeltaRampUp}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and either of size $1$ or indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals} (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then this \variable can also be indexed over \cstr{TimeHorizon}). This is meant to represent the vector \code{DP[ t ]} that, for each time instant $t$, contains the ramp-up value of the unit for the corresponding time step, i.e., the maximum possible increase of active power production w.r.t. the power that had been produced in time instant \code{t - 1}, if any. This variable is optional; if it is not provided then it is assumed that \code{DP[ t ] == MxP[ t ]}, i.e., the unit can ramp up by an arbitrary amount, i.e., there are no ramp-up constraints. If \cstr{DeltaRampUp} has length $1$ then \code{DP[ t ]} contains the same value for all $t$. Otherwise, \code{DeltaRampUp[ i ]} is the fixed value of \code{DP[ t ]} for all $t$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}. If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon}, then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{ChangeIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded.
%
 \item  the \variable \cstr{DeltaRampDown}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and either of size $1$ or indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals} (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then this \variable can also be indexed over \cstr{TimeHorizon}). This is meant to represent the vector \code{DM[ t ]} that, for each time instant $t$, contains the ramp-down value of the unit for the corresponding time step, i.e., the maximum possible decrease of active power production w.r.t. the power that had been produced in time instant \code{t - 1}, if any. This variable is optional; if it is not provided then it is assumed that \code{DM[ t ] == MxP[ t ]}, i.e., the unit can ramp down by an arbitrary amount, i.e., there are no ramp-down constraints. If \cstr{DeltaRampDown} has length $1$ then \code{DM[ t ]} contains the same value for all $t$. Otherwise, \code{DeltaRampDown[ i ]} is the fixed value of \code{DM[ t ]} for all $t$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}. If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon}, then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{ChangeIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded.
%
 \item the \variable \cstr{PrimaryRho}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and either of size $1$ or indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals} (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then this \variable can also be indexed over \cstr{TimeHorizon}). This is meant to represent the vector \code{PR[ t ]} that, for each time instant $t$, contains the maximum possible fraction of active power that can be used as primary reserve value of the unit for the corresponding time step. This variable is optional; if it is not provided then it is assumed that this unit may not be capable of producing any primary reserve, which correspond to \code{PR[ t ] == 0} for all $t$. If \cstr{PrimaryRho} has length $1$ then \code{PR[ t ]} contains the same value for all $t$. Otherwise, \code{PrimaryRho[ i ]} is the fixed value of \code{PR[ t ]} for all $t$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}. If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon}, then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{ChangeIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded.
 %
 \ \item the \variable \cstr{SecondaryRho}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and either of size $1$ or indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals} (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then this \variable can also be indexed over \cstr{TimeHorizon}). This is meant to represent the vector \code{SR[ t ]} that, for each time instant $t$, contains the maximum possible fraction of active power that can be used as secondary reserve value of the unit for the corresponding time step. This variable is optional; if it is not provided then it is assumed that this unit may not be capable of producing any secondary reserve, which correspond to \code{SR[ t ] == 0} for all $t$. If \cstr{SecondaryRho} has length $1$ then \code{SR[ t ]} contains the same value for all $t$. Otherwise, \code{SecondaryRho[ i ]} is the fixed value of \code{SR[ t ]} for all $t$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}. If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon}, then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{ChangeIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded.
 %
 \item the \variable \cstr{QuadTerm}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and either of size $1$ or indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals} (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then this \variable can also be indexed over \cstr{TimeHorizon}). This is meant to represent the vector \code{A[ t ]} that, for each time instant $t$, contains the quadratic term of power cost function of the unit for the corresponding time step. This variable is optional; if it is not provided then it is assumed that \code{A[ t ] == 0}, i.e., the cost of the unit is linear in the produced power. If \cstr{QuadTerm} has length $1$ then \code{A[ t ]} contains the same value for all $t$. Otherwise, \code{QuadTerm[ i ]} is the fixed value of \code{A[ t ]} for all $t$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}. If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon}, then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{ChangeIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded.
%
 \item the \variable \cstr{StartUpCost}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and either of size $1$ or indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals} (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then this \variable can also be indexed over \cstr{TimeHorizon}). This is meant to represent the vector \code{SC[ t ]} that, for each time instant $t$, contains the start up cost value of the unit for the corresponding time step. This variable is optional; if it is not provided then it is assumed that \code{SC[ t ] == 0}, i.e., this unit may not have any start up cost. If \cstr{StartUpCost} has length $1$ then \code{SC[ t ]} contains the same value for all $t$. Otherwise, \code{StartUpCost[ i ]} is the fixed value of \code{SC[ t ]} for all $t$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}. If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon}, then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{ChangeIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded.
 %
 \item the \variable \cstr{LinearTerm}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and either of size $1$ or indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals} (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then this \variable can also be indexed over \cstr{TimeHorizon}). This is meant to represent the vector \code{B[ t ]} that, for each time instant $t$, contains the linear term of power cost function of the unit for the corresponding time step. This variable is optional; if it is not provided then it is assumed that \code{B[ t ] == 0}, i.e., the cost of the unit has no linear dependence on the produced power (say, only the quadratic one). If \cstr{LinearTerm} has length $1$ then \code{B[ t ]} contains the same value for all $t$. Otherwise, \code{LinearTerm[ i ]} is the fixed value of \code{B[ t ]} for all $t$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}. If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon}, then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{ChangeIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded.
 %
 \item the \variable \cstr{ConstTerm}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and either of size $1$ or indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals} (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then this \variable can also be indexed over \cstr{TimeHorizon}). This is meant to represent the vector \code{C[ t ]} that, for each time instant $t$, contains the const term of power cost function of the unit for the corresponding time step. This variable is optional; if it is not provided then it is assumed that \code{C[ t ] == 0}, i.e.,  the cost of the unit has no fixed term, only those depending (linearly or quadratically) on the produced power. If \cstr{ConstTerm} has length $1$ then \code{C[ t ]} contains the same value for all $t$. Otherwise, \code{ConstTerm[ i ]} is the fixed value of \code{C[ t ]} for all $t$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}. If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon}, then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{ChangeIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded.
 %
 \item the scalar \variable \cstr{InitialPower}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and not indexed over any \dimension. This variable indicates the amount of the power that the unit was producing at time instant $-1$, i.e., before the start of the time horizon; this is necessary to compute the ramp-up and ramp-down constraints. Clearly, it must be that \code{MaxPower >= InitialPower >= MinPower} if the unit was ``on'' at time instant $-1$, and it must be that \code{InitialPower == 0} if the unit was ``off'' at time instant $-1$. The on/off status of the unit is also encoded by the scalar variable \code{InitUpDownTime}: in particular, \code{InitUpDownTime > 0} then the unit was on at time instant $-1$, and therefore \code{InitialPower >= MinPower} must hold, while if \code{InitUpDownTime <= 0} then the unit was off at time instant $-1$, and therefore \code{InitialPower == 0} by definition. In fact, if \code{InitUpDownTime <= 0} then this variable need not be defined since it is not loaded.
 %
 \item the scalar \variable \cstr{InitUpDownTime}, of type \code{netCDF::NcInt} and not indexed over any \dimension and indicates the initial time to generating the unit. If \code{InitUpDownTime > 0}, this means that the unit has been on for \code{InitUpDownTime} time stamps prior to time stamp $0$ (the beginning of the horizon). If, instead, \code{InitUpDownTime <= 0}, this means that the unit has been off for \code{- InitUpDownTime} time stamps prior to time stamp $0$; note that \code{InitUpDownTime == 0} means that the unit has been just shut down at the end of time instant $-1$, i.e., the beginning of time instant $0$.
 %
 \item the positive scalar \variable \cstr{MinUpTime}, of type \code{netCDF::NcUint} and not indexed over any \dimension, which indicates the minimum allowed up time in this unit. This variable is optional, if it is not provided it is taken to be \code{MinUpTime == 0}, which mean that the unit can shut down in the very same time stamp in which it starts up.
 %
 \item the positive scalar \variable \cstr{MinDownTime}, of type \code{netCDF::NcUint} and not indexed over any \dimension, which indicates the minimum allowed down time in this unit. This variable is optional, if it is not provided it is taken to be \code{MinDownTime == 0}, which mean that the unit can start up in the very same time stamp in which it starts up.
 %
 \item  the \variable \cstr{FixedConsumption}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and either of size $1$ or indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals} (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then this \variable can also be indexed over \cstr{TimeHorizon}). This is meant to represent the vector \code{FC[ t ]} that, for each time instant $t$, contains the fixed consumption of the power plant if it is OFF at time $t$. The variable is optional; if it is not defined, \code{FC[ t ] == 0} for all time instants. If it has size $1$, then \code{FC[ t ] == FixedConsumption[ 0 ]} for all $t$, regardless to what \cstr{NumberIntervals} says. Otherwise, \code{FixedConsumption[ i ]} is the fixed value of \code{FC[ t ]} for all $t$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ], ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}.
%
\item  the \variable \cstr{InertiaCommitment}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and either of size $1$ or indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals} (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then this \variable can also be indexed over \cstr{TimeHorizon}). This is meant to represent the vector \code{IC[ t ]} that, for each time instant $t$, contains the contribution that the unit can give to the inertia constraint for the sole fact that is is on (basically, the constant to be multiplied to the commitment variable) at time $t$. The variable is optional; if it is not defined, \code{IC[ t ] == 0} for all time instants. If it has size $1$, then \code{IC[ t ] == InertiaCommitment[ 0 ]} for all $t$, regardless to what \cstr{NumberIntervals} says. Otherwise, \code{InertiaCommitment[ i ]} is the fixed value of \code{IC[ t ]} for all $t$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ], ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}.

\end{itemize}

\subsubsection {\code{HydroUnitBlock}}\label{sec:HydroUnitBlock}
The \code{HydroUnitBlock} class derives from \code{UnitBlock} and implements a \cstr{reasonably standard} hydro unit of a Unit Commitment Problem. That is, the class is designed in order to give mathematical formulation to describe the operation of large set of hydro storage. To model complex reservoir systems several technical parameters have to be considered. These are divided into reservoir-specific parameters, the hydro links connecting the reservoirs and finally the turbine/pump parameters. The values are collected within a reservoir database, a hydro-link database and a turbine/pump-database.
Besides the mandatory \cstr{type} attribute of any \dBlock, the group must contain all the data required by the base \code{UnitBlock}, as described in the comments to \code{UnitBlock::deserialize}(\code{netCDF::NcGroup}). In particular, we refer to that description for the crucial \dimensions \cstr{TimeHorizon}, \cstr{NumberIntervals} and \cstr{ChangeIntervals}. The \code{netCDF::NcGroup} must then also contain:
%
\begin{itemize}
 \item The \dimension \cstr{NumberReservoirs} containing the number of all reservoirs (or nodes) in the \code{HydroUnitBlock}. The \dimension is optional, if it is not provided then it is taken to be \code{== 1}, which means that the (in principle) cascading hydro system is actually single hydro reservoir. Note, however, that a single reservoir can still have multiple hydro generating units (see \cstr{NumberArcs} below).
 %
 \item The \dimension \cstr{NumberArcs} containing the set of arcs (or units) connecting the reservoirs in cascading system. Each arc represents either a turbine generating electricity by converting potential energy of water going downhill, or a pump consuming electricity for moving water uphill.
 %
 \item The \variable \cstr{StartArc}, of type \code{netCDF::NcUint} and indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberArcs}; the r-th entry of the variable is the starting point of the arc (a number in \code{0, \dots, NumberReservoirs - 1}). Note that arcs are oriented; that is, a positive flow along arc $r$ (turbine) means that water is being taken away from \code{StartArc[ r ]} and delivered to \code{EndArc[ r ]} (see next), a negative flow (pump) means vice-versa. Note that reservoir names here go from $0$ to \code{NumberReservoirs - 1}.
 %
 \item The \variable \cstr{EndArc}, of type \code{netCDF::NcUint} and indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberArcs}; the r-th entry of the variable is the ending point of the arc; this is a number in \code{0, \dots, NumberReservoirs}. Note: this is \code{NumberReservoirs} and \highlight{not} \code{NumberReservoirs - 1}, because arcs can end in the ``fake'' reservoir \code{NumberReservoirs}. This indicates that water that flows along that arc ``goes away from the system'' and it is no longer counted, because it can no longer be used to produce electricity further down the river, or pumped back into one of its reservoirs. Indeed, there will be something like ``the most downstream turbines'': after water has been used there, it just goes away down some river and does not go to any other reservoir. Arcs are oriented (see above); \code{StartArc[ r ] == EndArc[ r ]} (a self-loop) is not allowed, but multiple arcs between the same pair of reservoirs are. Indeed, often the same physical equipment can be used both as a turbine and as a pump; in our model these are represented as two parallel arcs (but with different upper and lower flow capacity, see \cstr{MinFlow} and \cstr{MaxFlow} below).
 %
 \item The \variable \cstr{MinFlow}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and indexed over both \dimensions \cstr{NumberIntervals} and \cstr{NumberArcs}. The first \dimension may have either size $1$ or size \code{NumberIntervals} (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then the size can also be \cstr{TimeHorizon}) whereas the second one always has size \cstr{NumberArcs} (if the variable is provided at all). This is meant to represent the matrix \code{MinF[ t , l ]} which, for each time instant $t$ and each arc $l$, contains the minimum flow value of the unit. This variable is optional; if it is not provided then it is assumed that \code{MinF[ t , l ]== 0}, i.e., the minimum flow of the unit is zero. If the first \dimension has size $1$ then the entry \code{MinF[ 0 , l ]} gives the fixed minimum flow value of the unit for all time steps and each arc $l$. Otherwise, \code{MinFlow[ i , l ]} is the fixed value of \code{MinF[ t , l ]} for all time $t$ and arc $l$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ]= 0}. If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon}, then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{ChangeIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded.
 %
 \item The \variable \cstr{MaxFlow}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and indexed over both \dimensions \cstr{NumberIntervals} and \cstr{NumberArcs}. The first \dimension may have either size $1$ or size \code{NumberIntervals}  (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then the size can also be \cstr{TimeHorizon}) whereas the second one always has size \cstr{NumberArcs} (if the variable is provided at all). This is meant to represent the matrix \code{MaxF[ t , l ]} which, for each time instant $t$ and each arc $l$, contains the maximum flow value of the unit. This variable is optional; if it is not provided then it is assumed that \code{MaxF[ t , l ]== 0}, i.e., the maximum flow of the unit is zero. If the first \dimension has size 1 then the entry \code{MaxF[ 0 , l ]} gives the fixed maximum flow value of the unit for all time steps and each arc $l$. Otherwise, \code{MaxFlow[ i , l ]} is the fixed value of \code{MaxF[ t , l ]} for all time $t$ and arc $l$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ]= 0}. If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon}, then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{ChangeIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded.
\end{itemize}
\smallskip
\noindent
Note: \cstr{MinFlow} and \cstr{MaxFlow} values can be either positive or negative (or zero); whenever \code{MinF[ t , l ] < MaxF[ t , l ] <= 0} for each $t$ and $l$, the unit is considered a pump and whenever \code{0 <= MinF[ t , l ] < MaxF[ t , l ]}, the unit is considered a turbine. Note that an arc must \highlight{always} be the same kind for \highlight{all} instants, i.e., it is not allowed that a unit suddenly changes between a turbine and a pump, or vice-versa. This is because the flow-to-active-power function of turbines is a convex piecewise function with possibly many pieces (see \cstr{NumberPieces}, \cstr{LinearTerm}, \cstr{ConstantTerm} below) , whereas the flow-to-active-power function of a pump is a simple linear function. In other words, the ``number of pieces'' of a turbine is \code{>= 1}, whereas the ``number of pieces'' of a pump is necessarily equal to $1$. In reality, the same equipment can sometimes be used both as a pump and as a turbine. In our model this is be accounted for by artificially splitting the unit into \cstr{two units}, a pump one and a turbine one, which must be done at the data processing stage. This causes the possible problem that at some time instant both the pump and the turbine be active, which is not possible in practice. This is unlikely to happen (because pumps consume more than turbines produce for the same amount of water, so this would be uneconomical), but should it ever happen, this occurrence is not handled in our model (which lets it happen).

\begin{itemize}
 \item The \variable \cstr{MinVolumetric}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and indexed over both \dimensions \cstr{NumberReservoirs} and \cstr{NumberIntervals}. The first \dimension always has size \cstr{NumberReservoirs} (if it is provided at all), whereas the second one may have size one or size \cstr{NumberIntervals}  (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then the size can also be \cstr{TimeHorizon}). This is meant to represent the matrix \code{MinV[ r , t ]} which, for each reservoir $r$ at each time instant $t$ contains the minimum volumetric value of the unit for each reservoir and corresponding time step. It must be that \code{0 <= MinV[ r , t ] < MaxV[ r , t ]} for all $r$ and $t$ and . This variable is optional; if it's not provided then it is assumed that \code{MinV[ r , t ] == 0}, i.e., the minimum volumetric of the unit is zero. If the second \dimension has size 1 then the entry \code{MinV[ r , 0 ]} gives the fixed minimum volumetric value of the unit for each reservoir $r$ along all the time horizon. Otherwise, \code{MinVolumetric[ r , i ]} is the fixed value of \code{MinV[ r , t ]} for reservoir $r$ and all $t$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}. If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon}, then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{ChangeIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded.
 %
 \item The \variable \cstr{MaxVolumetric}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and indexed over both \dimensions \cstr{NumberReservoirs} and \cstr{NumberIntervals}. The first \dimension always has size \cstr{NumberReservoirs} (if it is provided at all), whereas the second one may have size one or size \cstr{NumberIntervals}  (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then the size can also be \cstr{TimeHorizon}). This is meant to represent the matrix \code{MaxV[ r , t ]} which, for each reservoir $r$ at each time instant $t$ contains the maximum volumetric value of the unit for each reservoir and corresponding time step. It must be that \code{0 <= MinV[ r , t ] < MaxV[ r , t ]} for all $r$ and $t$ and . This variable is optional; if it's not provided then it is assumed that \code{MaxV[ r , t ] == 0}, i.e., the maximum volumetric of the unit is zero. If the second \dimension has size 1 then the entry \code{MaxV[ r , 0 ]} gives the fixed maximum volumetric value of the unit for each reservoir $r$ along all the time horizon. Otherwise, \code{MaxVolumetric[ r , i ]} is the fixed value of \code{MaxV[ r , t ]} for reservoir $r$ and all $t$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}. If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon}, then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{ChangeIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded.
\end{itemize}
Note: It may happen that \code{MinV[ r , t ] == MaxV[ r , t ]}, but only \highlight{in a subset of the time instants}. For instance, the user may want to fix the final value of the reservoir, for whatever reason. So, if \code{MinV} and \code{MaxV} are independent of t, then \code{MinV[ r ] < MaxV[ r ]} must surely happen. If, instead, they depend on $t$, then equality can be accepted at some instants (but not all of them).
\smallskip
\noindent
Besides the mandatory \cstr{type} attribute of any \dBlock, the group must contain all the data required by the base \code{UnitBlock}, as described in the comments to \code{UnitBlock::deserialize}( \code{netCDF::NcGroup} ). In particular, we refer to that description for the crucial \dimensions \cstr{TimeHorizon}, \cstr{NumberIntervals} and \cstr{ChangeIntervals}. The \code{netCDF::NcGroup} must then also contain:
\begin{itemize}

 \item The \variable \cstr{Inflows}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and indexed over both \dimensions \cstr{NumberReservoirs} and \cstr{TimeHorizon}. This is meant to represent the matrix \code{InF[ r , t ]} which, for each reservoir $r$, contains the amount of water that ``naturally'' enters into reservoir $r$ (because of rain, ice melting, non-controlled rivers flowing, and of course net of water leaving by evaporation, human consumption etc.) during the time interval $t$, and therefore that is available in the reservoir at the end of time step $t$ (hence, the beginning of time step $t + 1$, if any). This variable is optional; if it isn't defined, it is taken to be zero. \cstr{Inflows} can be either positive or negative.
 %
 \item The \variable \cstr{MinPower}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and indexed over both \dimensions \cstr{NumberIntervals} and \cstr{NumberArcs}. The first \dimension may have either size $1$ or size \cstr{NumberIntervals}  (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then the size can also be \cstr{TimeHorizon}), whereas the second one always has size \cstr{NumberArcs} (if it is provided at all). This is meant to represent the matrix \code{MinP[ t , l ]} which, for each time instant $t$ at each arc $l$ contains the minimum power value of the unit; it must be that \code{MinP[ t , l ] < MaxP[ t , l ]} for each time instant $t$ and each arc $l$. This variable is optional; if it is not provided then it is assumed that \code{MinP[ t , l ] == 0}, i.e., the minimum power of all units is zero(which means, each unit is a turbine). If the first \dimension has size $1$ then the entry \code{MinP[ 0 , l ]} is assumed to contain the minimum power of arc $l$ for all time instants. Otherwise, \code{MinPower[ i , l ]} is the fixed value of \code{MinP[ t , l ]} for arc $l$ and all time $t$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}. If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon}, then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{NumberIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded.
 %
 \item The \variable \cstr{MaxPower}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and indexed over both \dimensions \cstr{NumberIntervals} and \cstr{NumberArcs}. The first \dimension may have either size $1$ or size \cstr{NumberIntervals}  (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then the size can also be \cstr{TimeHorizon}), whereas the second one always has size \cstr{NumberArcs} (if it is provided at all). This is meant to represent the matrix \code{MaxP[ t , l ]} which, for each time instant $t$ at each arc $l$ contains the maximum power value of the unit; it must be that \code{MinP[ t , l ] < MaxP[ t , l ]} for each time instant $t$ and each arc $l$. This variable is optional; if it is not provided then it is assumed that \code{MaxP[ t , l ] == 0}, i.e., the maximum power of all units is zero(which means, each unit is a turbine). If the first \dimension has size $1$ then the entry \code{MaxP[ 0 , l ]} is assumed to contain the maximum power of arc $l$ for all time instants. Otherwise, \code{MaxPower[ i , l ]} is the fixed value of \code{MaxP[ t , l ]} for arc $l$ and all time $t$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}. If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon}, then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{NumberIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded.
%
 \item The \variable \cstr{DeltaRampUp}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and indexed over both \dimensions \cstr{NumberIntervals} and \cstr{NumberArcs}. The first \dimension may have either size $1$ or size \cstr{NumberIntervals} (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then the size can also be \cstr{TimeHorizon}), whereas the second one always has size \cstr{NumberArcs}  (if it is provided at all). This is meant to represent the matrix \code{DP[ t , l ]} which contains the maximum possible increase of the flow rate at time instant $t$ for arc $l$. This variable is optional; if it is not provided then it is assumed that \code{DP[ t , l ] == MaxP[ t , l ] - MinP[ t , l ]}, i.e., all units can ramp up by an arbitrary amount, i.e., there are no ramp-up constraints. If the first \dimension has size 1 then the entry \code{DP[ 0 , l ]} is assumed to contain the maximum possible increase of the flow rate of arc $l$ for all time instants. Otherwise, \code{DeltaRampUp[ i , l ]} is the fixed value of \code{DP[ t , l ]} for arc $l$ and all time $t$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}. If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon}, then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{NumberIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded.
 %
 \item The \variable \cstr{DeltaRampDown}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and indexed over both \dimensions \cstr{NumberIntervals} and \cstr{NumberArcs}. The first \dimension may have either size $1$ or size \cstr{NumberIntervals} (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then the size can also be \cstr{TimeHorizon}), whereas the second one always has size \cstr{NumberArcs} (if it is provided at all). This is meant to represent the matrix \code{DM[ t , l ]} which contains the maximum possible decrease of the flow rate at each time instant $t$ of each arc $l$. This variable is optional; if it is not provided then it is assumed that \code{DM[ t , l ] == MaxP[ t , l ] - MinP[ t , l ]}, i.e., the unit can ramp down by an arbitrary amount, i.e., there are no ramp-down constraints. If first \dimension has size $1$ then the entry \code{DM[ 0 , l ]} is assumed to contain the maximum possible decrease of the flow rate of arc $l$ for all time instants. Otherwise, \code{DeltaRampDown[ i , l ]} is the fixed value of \code{DM[ t , l ]} for arc $l$ and all time $t$  in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}. If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon}, then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{NumberIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded.
 %
 \item The \variable \cstr{PrimaryRho}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and indexed both over the \dimensions \cstr{NumberIntervals} and \cstr{NumberArcs}. The first \dimension may have either size $1$ or size \cstr{NumberIntervals} (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then the size can also be \cstr{TimeHorizon}), whereas the second one always has size \cstr{NumberArcs} (if it is provided at all). This is meant to represent the matrix \code{PR[ t , l ]} which, for each time instant $t$ and arc $l$, contains the maximum possible fraction of active power that can be used as primary reserve. This variable is optional, when it's not present then \code{PR[ t , l ] == 0} for all $t$ and $l$, i.e., the unit is not capable of producing any primary reserve. Note that only turbines can produce primary reserve, i.e., \code{PR[ t , l ] > 0 ==> MaxP[ t , l ] > 0}. If the first \dimension has size $1$ then the entry \code{PR[ 0 , l ]} is assumed to contain the maximum possible fraction of active power that can be used as primary reserve by arc $l$ for all time instants. Otherwise, \code{PrimaryRho[ i , l ]} is the fixed value of \code{PR[ t , l ]} for arc $l$ and all $t$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}. If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon}, then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{NumberIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded.
 %
 \item The \variable \cstr{SecondaryRho}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and indexed both over the \dimensions \cstr{NumberIntervals} and \cstr{NumberArcs}. The first \dimension may have either size $1$ or size \cstr{NumberIntervals} (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then the size can also be \cstr{TimeHorizon}), whereas the second one always has size \cstr{NumberArcs} (if it is provided at all). This is meant to represent the matrix \code{SR[ t , l ]} which, for each time instant $t$ and arc $l$, contains the maximum possible fraction of active power that can be used as secondary reserve. This variable is optional, when it's not present then \code{SR[ t , l ] == 0} for all $t$ and $l$, i.e., the unit is not capable of producing any secondary reserve. Note that only turbines can produce secondary reserve, i.e., \code{SR[ t , l ] > 0 ==> MaxP[ t , l ] > 0}. If the first \dimension has size $1$ then the entry \code{SR[ 0 , l ]} is assumed to contain the maximum possible fraction of active power that can be used as secondary reserve by arc $l$ for all time instants. Otherwise, \code{SecondaryRho[ i , l ]} is the fixed value of \code{SR[ t , l ]} for arc $l$ and all $t$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}. If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon}, then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{NumberIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded.
 %
 \item The \variable \cstr{NumberPieces}, of type \code{netCDF::NcUint} and indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberArcs}. \code{NumberPieces[ l ]} tells how many pieces the concave flow-to-active-power function has for unit (arc) $l$. Note that pumps must necessarily have exactly one piece. The sum over all i of \code{NumberPieces[ i ]} is the total number of pieces (say, \cstr{TotalNumberPieces}). Clearly, \code{TotalNumberPieces >= NumberArcs}; if the flow-to-active-power function for all turbines only have one piece (those of pumps necessarily are so), then \code{TotalNumberPieces == NumberArcs} and there is no need to define this variable. If, instead, if it is defined, then should always be such that \code{TotalNumberPieces >= NumberArcs}.
 %
 \item The \variable \cstr{LinearTerm}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and indexed over the set \code{\{0 , \dots, TotalNumberPieces - 1\}} (see \cstr{NumberPieces}). \code{LinearTerm[ h ]} gives the linear term $a_h$ of the linear function $a_h f + b_h$ that defines the concave flow-to-active-power function for some unit; the total function if $F2AP( f ) = \min \{ a_h f + b_h , h \in H \}$ for some finite set $H$ that depends on the individual unit. It is then necessary to be able to assign a unique index \code{h = 0, 1, \dots, TotalNumberPieces - 1} to each pair ( unit , linear function $a_h f + b_h$ ). When \code{TotalNumberPieces == NumberArcs}, the index is the same as \code{i = 0, 1, \dots, NumberArcs - 1} (there is a one-to-one correspondence between each (unit) arc and each piece). When, instead, \code{TotalNumberPieces > NumberArcs}, a mapping must be defined. The mapping is the obvious one: each index of \code{i = 0, 1, \dots, NumberArcs - 1}, corresponds with a unit (arc), and the linear functions for each unit (arc) also have some natural ordering. Thus, in general the mapping is:
 \begin{itemize}
  \item piece $0$ = first piece of unit (arc) $0$
  \item piece $1$ = second piece of unit (arc) $0$
  \item \code{\dots}
  \item piece \code{NumberPieces[ 0 ] - 1 =} last piece of unit (arc) $0$
  \item piece \code{NumberPieces[ 0 ] =} first piece of unit (arc) $1$
  \item piece \code{NumberPieces[ 0 ] + 1 =} second piece of unit (arc) $1$
  \item \code{\dots}
 \end{itemize}
 which of course boils down to \cstr{h = i} when each arc has exactly one piece.
 \item The \variable \cstr{ConstantTerm}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and indexed over the set \code{\{0 , \dots, TotalNumberPieces - 1\}}. \code{ConstantTerm[ h ]} gives the constant term $b_h$ of the linear function $a_h f + b_h$ that defines the concave flow-to-active-power function for some unit; see the comments to \cstr{LinearTerm} for details.
 %
 \item The \variable \cstr{InertiaPower}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and indexed both over the \dimensions \cstr{NumberIntervals} and \cstr{NumberArcs}. The first \dimension may have either size $1$ or size \cstr{NumberIntervals}  (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then the size can also be \cstr{TimeHorizon}) whereas the second one always has size \cstr{NumberArcs} (if it is provided at all). This is meant to represent the matrix \code{IP[ t , l ]} which, for each time instant $t$ and arc $l$, contains the contribution that the unit can give to the inertia constraint which depends on the active power that it is currently generating (basically, the constant to be multiplied to the active power variable) at time $t$ for arc $l$. The variable is optional; if it is not defined, \code{IP[ t , l ] == 0} for each time instants $t$ and arc $l$. If the first \dimension has size $1$ then the entry \code{IP[ 0 , l ]} is assumed to contain the inertia power value for arc $l$ and all time instants $t$. Otherwise, \code{InertiaPower[ i , l ]} is the fixed value of \code{IP[ t , l ]} for all $t$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ]= 0} and all $l$. If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon} then the mapping clearly does not require \code{ChangeIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded.
 %
 \item The \variable \cstr{InitialFlowRate}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberArcs}. Each entry \code{InFR[ i ]} indicates the amount of the flow that was going along arc $i$ at time instant $-1$. This is necessary to compute ramp-up and ramp-down limits (cf. \cstr{DeltaRampUp} and \cstr{DeltaRampDown}), and therefore it is useless if there are no ramp constraints on \highlight{any} unit (arc), in which case it is not loaded.
 %
 \item The \variable \cstr{InitialVolumetric}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberReservoirs}. Each entry \code{InV[ r ]} indicates the volumes of water in reservoir $r$ at time instant $-1$.
 %
 \item The negative or positive scalar \variable \cstr{UphillFlow}, of type \code{netCDF::NcInt} and indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberArcs}. Each entry \code{UpF[ l ]} indicates the uphill flow delay for each unit (arc) $l$; the nontrivial concept is detailed below. This variable is optional, if it is not provided it is taken to be \code{UpF[ l ] == 0}.
 %
 \item The positive scalar \variable \cstr{DownhillFlow}, of type \code{netCDF::NcUint} and indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberArcs}. Each entry \code{DnF[ l ]} indicates the downhill flow delay for each arc (unit) $l$. This variable is optional, if it is not provided it is taken to be \code{DnF[ l ] == 0}.
\end{itemize}

The last two quantities require some comment. Let us assume that we have any arc $l$, with \code{( StartArc[ l ] = n , EndArc[ l ] = n' )}, which corresponds to (say) a turbine. This means that a positive flow along $l$ implies that water is being taken away from $n$ and delivered to $n'$, passing through the turbine to produce flow, as graphically depicted below:
\\
\\

$  \backslash n / >==============> [ TURBINE ] >================>  \backslash n' / $
\\

\hspace{25mm}  $ UpF[ l ]$    \hspace{53mm}     $ DnF[ l ]$
\\

The issue here is that the turbine can be geographically far enough from both $n$ and $n'$ that the water can take a long time (one or more time instant, especially if these are \cstr{short} such as $15$ or $5$ minutes) to reach the turbine from $n$ and $n'$ from the turbine.
\\
A particular note of caution has to be mentioned regarding the fact that \code{UpF[ l ]} can be \highlight{negative}: this means that the water is used in the turbine \highlight{before} it goes out of reservoir $n$. This is counter-intuitive, but can be explained by the fact that the pipe between $n$ and the turbine can be full, and therefore works as a \cstr{mini reservoir} in itself. When the turbine is started, a \cstr{bubble} (depression) is created uphill the turbine; this \cstr{bubbles up} the $n ==> TURBINE$ pipe until it reaches $n$, and it is only at that point that the water in $n$ starts flowing away. Hence, there is a negative temporal delay between the water starting flowing in the turbine and it starting flowing away from n. Note that if the $n ==> TURBINE$ pipe is rather empty, the delay is positive in that one have to start sending the water, which may take some time before filling the pipe and therefore starting the turbine. Of course these are all somewhat crude approximations of the true physical behavior, but they are accurate enough for this setting. Yet, the case \code{UpF[ l ] < 0} cannot be disregarded.

\subsubsection {\code{BatteryUnitBlock}}
The \code{BatteryUnitBlock} class derives from \code{UnitBlock} and implements a \cstr{reasonably standard} Battery storage, E-mobility, Centralized demand response, Distributed load management, Distributed storage, and Power to gas units in a single class at Unit commitment problem. This can be the case of actual physical batteries, either \cstr{large} (battery storage) or \cstr{small} (e-mobility, distributed storage), of methods that use some intermediate energy vector with limited local storage/production (power-to-gas units), as well as of \cstr{logical} mechanisms that allow to temporally shift production/consumption in a limited way, thereby acting like an energy storage (centralized demand response, distributed load management). \code{BatteryUnitBlock} provides a quite general concept of \cstr{battery} that covers different units which mostly fit the same mathematical equations pattern. For instance, a \code{BatteryUnitBlock} may or may not have a fixed demand (e-mobility has, other units have not) and it may or may not provide primary and secondary reserve (battery storage may do, but other units don't). Battery storage provide an additional flexibility to the system by shifting a surplus of electric energy (e.g. due to high renewable feeding) to times with high demand or lower renewable generation. The distributed battery storage can be aggregated in the energy cells or directly placed in a single node of the network. We will therefore not stress this dependency in the subsequent equations. We emphasize that potential contribution of batteries to inertia is still a subject of active research and should be considered as optional. Besides, since the transport sector is moving towards electrification, electric mobility will have a rising impact on the electricity system. First, electricity demand is growing due to a higher amount of electric vehicles that need to be charged. On the other hand, vehicles are used only a small amount of time while being charged over a much longer timespan (e.g. at night). This allows to shift the charging process in time and provide this flexibility to the overall energy system by means of an additional generator (vehicle-to-grid) or an additional load (power-to-vehicle). Two main differences between battery storages unit and other existing units in this class are:
\begin{itemize}
 \item battery storages unit can do primary and secondary reserve, while other units cannot;
 \item some of the units may have a fixed demand that battery storages unit has not.
\end{itemize}
Moreover, as the considered storage cycle is small w.r.t. the EUC time horizon, distributed storage is not considered as seasonal storage. Hence, the associated mathematical description follows the same equations as the one provided for battery storages unit. The specificity of distributed storage only relies on the fact that it is connected to a distribution grid node.
\smallskip
\noindent
\begin{itemize}
\item The \variable \cstr{MinStorage}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and either of size $1$ or indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals}  (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then this \variable can also be indexed over \cstr{TimeHorizon}). This is meant to represent the vector \code{MinS[ t ]} that, for each time instant $t$, contains the minimum storage level of the unit for the corresponding time step. If \cstr{MinStorage} has length $1$ then \code{MinS[ t ]} contains the same value for all $t$. Otherwise, \code{MinStorage[ i ]} is the fixed value of \code{MinS[ t ]} for all $t$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}. Note that it must always be \code{0 <= MinS[ t ] < MaxS[ t ]} for all $t$. If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon}, then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{ChangeIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded.
%
\item The \variable \cstr{MaxStorage}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and either of size $1$ or indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals}  (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then this \variable can also be indexed over \cstr{TimeHorizon}). This is meant to represent the vector \code{MaxS[ t ]} that, for each time instant $t$, contains the maximum storage level of the unit for the corresponding time step. If \cstr{MaxStorage} has length $1$ then \code{MaxS[ t ]} contains the same value for all $t$. Otherwise, \code{MaxStorage[ i ]} is the fixed value of \code{MaxS[ t ]} for all $t$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}. Note that it must always be \code{[0 <=] MinS[ t ] < MaxS[ t ]} for all $t$. If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon}, then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{ChangeIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded.
%
\item The \variable \cstr{MinPower}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and either of size $1$ or indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals} (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then this \variable can also be indexed over \cstr{TimeHorizon}). This is meant to represent the vector \code{MinP[ t ]} that, for each time instant $t$, contains the minimum active power output value of the unit for the corresponding time step. If \cstr{MinPower} has length $1$ then \code{MinP[ t ]} contains the same value for all $t$. Otherwise, \code{MinPower[ i ]} is the fixed value of \code{MinP[ t ]} for all $t$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}. Note that it must be \code{MinP[ t ] <= 0} for all $t$. If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon}, then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{ChangeIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded.
%
\item The \variable \cstr{MaxPower}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and either of size $1$ or indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals} (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then this \variable can also be indexed over \cstr{TimeHorizon}). This is meant to represent the vector \code{MaxP[ t ]} that, for each time instant $t$, contains the maximum active power output value of the unit for the corresponding time step. If \cstr{MaxPower} has length $1$ then \code{MaxP[ t ]} contains the same value for all $t$. Otherwise, \code{MaxPower[ i ]} is the fixed value of \code{MaxP[ t ]} for all $t$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}. Note that it must be \code{MinP[ t ] <= 0} for all $t$. If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon}, then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{ChangeIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded.
%
\item The scalar \variable \cstr{InitialPower}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and not indexed over any \dimension. This variable indicates the amount of the power that the unit was producing at time instant $-1$, i.e., before the start of the time horizon; this is necessary to compute the ramp-up and ramp-down constraints. This variable is optional; if \cstr{DeltaRampUp} and \cstr{DeltaRampDown} are not present, \cstr{InitialPower} should not be read, since there are no ramping constraints. If \cstr{DeltaRampUp} and \cstr{DeltaRampDown} are present but \cstr{InitialPower} is not provided, its value is taken to be $0$.
%
\item The \variable \cstr{MaxPrimaryPower}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and either of size $1$ or indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals} (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then this \variable can also be indexed over \cstr{TimeHorizon}). This is meant to represent the vector \code{MaxPP[ t ]} that, for each time instant $t$, contains the maximum active power that can be used as primary reserve of the unit for the corresponding time step. If \cstr{MaxPrimaryPower} has length $1$ then \code{MaxPP[ t ]} contains the same value for all $t$. Otherwise, \code{MaxPrimaryPower[ i ]} is the fixed value of \code{MaxPP[ t ]} for all $t$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}. This variable is optional, if is not provided then \code{MaxPP[ t ] == 0} for all $t$. If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon}, then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{ChangeIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded.
%
\item The \variable \cstr{MaxSecondaryPower}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and either of size $1$ or indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals} (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then this \variable can also be indexed over \cstr{TimeHorizon}). This is meant to represent the vector \code{MaxSP[ t ]} that, for each time instant $t$, contains the maximum active power that can be used as secondary reserve of the unit for the corresponding time step. If \cstr{MaxSecondaryPower} has length $1$ then \code{MaxSP[ t ]} contains the same value for all $t$. Otherwise, \code{MaxSecondaryPower[ i ]} is the fixed value of \code{MaxSP[ t ]} for all $t$ in the interval\code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}. This variable is optional, if is not provided then \code{MaxSP[ t ] == 0} for all $t$. Note that \code{MaxPP[ t ] == 0} implies \code{MaxSP[ t ] == 0} (that is, if \code{MaxPrimaryPower} is not defined then neither should \code{MaxSecondaryPower}). If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon}, then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{ChangeIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded.
%
\item The \variable \cstr{DeltaRampUp}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and either of size $1$ or indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals} (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then this \variable can also be indexed over \cstr{TimeHorizon}). This is meant to represent the vector \code{DP[ t ]} that, for each time instant $t$, contains the ramp-up value of the unit for the corresponding time step, i.e., the maximum possible increase of active power production w.r.t. the power that had been produced in time instant $t - 1$, if any. If \cstr{DeltaRampUp} has length $1$ then \code{DP[ t ]} contains the same value for all $t$. Otherwise, \code{DeltaRampUp[ i ]} is the fixed value of \code{DP[ t ]} for all $t$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}. This variable is optional; if it is not provided then it is assumed that \code{DP[ t ] == MaxP[ t ]}, i.e., the unit can ramp up by an arbitrary amount, i.e., there are no ramp-up constraints. If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon}, then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{ChangeIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded.
%
\item The \variable \cstr{DeltaRampDown}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and either of size $1$ or indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals} (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then this \variable can also be indexed over \cstr{TimeHorizon}). This is meant to represent the vector \code{DM[ t ]} that, for each time instant $t$, contains the ramp-down value of the unit for the corresponding time step, i.e., the maximum possible decrease of active power production w.r.t. the power that had been produced in time instant $t - 1$, if any. If \cstr{DeltaRampDown} has length $1$ then \code{DM[ t ]} contains the same value for all $t$. Otherwise, \code{DeltaRampDown[ i ]} is the fixed value of \code{DM[ t ]} for all t in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}. This variable is optional; if it is not provided then it is assumed that \code{DM[ t ] == MaxP[ t ]}, i.e., the unit can ramp down an arbitrary amount, i.e., there are no ramp-down constraints. If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon}, then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{ChangeIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded.
%
 \item The \variable \cstr{StoringBatteryRho}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and to be either of size $1$ or indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals} (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then this \variable can also be indexed over \cstr{TimeHorizon}). This is meant to represent the vector \code{SBR[ t ]} that, for each time instant $t$, contains the inefficiency of storing energy of the unit for the corresponding time step. This variable is optional; if it is not provided then it is assumed that \code{SBR[ t ] == 1} for all $t$, i.e., no (significant) energy is spent just for storing it in the battery (this simplifies the model somewhat, see below). If \cstr{StoringBatteryRho} has length $1$ then \code{SBR[ t ]} contains the same value for all $t$. Otherwise, \code{StoringBatteryRho[ i ]} is the fixed value of \code{SBR[ t ]} for all $t$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]} with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}.
