Note that it must be always such that \code{SBR[ t ] <= 1 for all t (as SBR[ t ]} is the amount of energy actually going in the battery for each 1 unit of input energy). If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon}, then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{ChangeIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded.
 %
 \item The \variable \cstr{ExtractingBatterRho}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and to be either of size $1$ or indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals} (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then this \variable can also be indexed over \cstr{TimeHorizon}). This is meant to represent the vector \code{EBR[ t ]} that, for each time instant $t$, contains the inefficiency of extracting energy of the unit for the corresponding time step. This variable is optional; if it is not provided, then it is assumed that \code{EBR[ t ] == 1} for all $t$, i.e., no (significant) energy is spent just for extracting it from the battery (this simplifies the model somewhat, see below). If \cstr{ExtractingBatterRho} has length $1$ then \code{EBR[ t ]} contains the same value for all $t$. Otherwise, \code{ExtractingBatterRho[ i ]} is the fixed value of \code{EBR[ t ]} for all $t$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]} with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}. Note that it must be always such that \code{EBR[ t ] >= 1 [>= SBR[ t ]] for all t (as EBR[ t ]} is the amount of energy that is taken away from the battery to obtain 1 unit of output energy). If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon}, then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{ChangeIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded.
\end{itemize}
Note: the special case in which \code{EBR[ t ] == SBR[ t ] == 1} for all $t$, i.e., no energy is spent for storing it in / retrieving it from the battery, leads to significantly simpler mathematical models. In particular, one single variable can be used to represent both storing and retrieving, rather than requiring two separate ones (unless primary and secondary reserve are allowed and/or the cost is defined, since this also requires using two), and the binary variables need not to be defined. For details, see the comments to \code{generate\_abstract\_variable()} and \code{generate\_abstract\_constraints()}.
\begin{itemize}
 \item The scalar \variable \cstr{InitialStorage}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and not indexed over any \dimension. This variable indicates the amount of the storage level that the unit was producing at time instant $-1$, i.e., before the start of the time horizon; this is necessary to compute the storage level connection with intake and outtake constraints.
 %
 \item The \variable \cstr{Cost}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and either of size $1$ or indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals} (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then this \variable can also be indexed over \cstr{TimeHorizon}). This is meant to represent the vector \code{C[ t ]} that, for each time instant $t$, contains the monetary cost of storing one unit or energy into, or extracting it from, the battery (the cost is the same in both cases) at the corresponding time step. This variable is optional; if it is not provided then it's taken to be zero. If \cstr{Cost} has length $1$ then \code{C[ t ]} contains the same value for all $t$. Otherwise, \code{Cost[ i ]} is the fixed value of \code{C[ t ]} for all $t$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}. If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon}, then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{ChangeIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded.
 %
 \item The \variable \cstr{Demand}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and indexed over the \dimension \cstr{TimeHorizon}: the entry \code{Demand[ t ]} is assumed to contain the amount of energy that must be discharged from the battery and \cstr{sent away for some other purpose} (say, driving your e-car) at time t. This variable is optional; if it isn't defined, then \code{Demand[ t ] == 0}. Otherwise the \code{Demand[ t ]} contains the demand value for each time instant $t$.
%
\item The scalar \variable \cstr{Kappa}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble}. This
  \variable contains the factor that multiplies the minimum and maximum active
  power, maximum primary and secondary reserve, and the minimum and maximum
  storage levels, at each time instant. This \variable is optional, if it is
  not provided it is taken to be \code{Kappa == 1}.
\end{itemize}

\subsubsection {\code{IntermittentUnitBlock}}
The \code{IntermittentUnitBlock} class derives from \code{UnitBlock} and implements a Intermittent Generation for a unit representing generation (be it centralized or distributed) by intermittent (= unreliable) sources in the unit commitment problem, such as wind farms, solar parks and run-of-the-river hydroelectricity. Each unit is supposed to be connected to a specific node of the clustered network (which means that the \cstr{distributed} case refers to \cstr{distributed in a small region}, where of course \cstr{small} depends on the granularity of the network description. The model relies mainly on historical data of local generation of wind and solar at each node of the grid; these data are used to develop normalized generation profiles associated with wind and solar generators. Intermittent generators are supposed to be able to contribute to primary and secondary reserves. Contribution to the system inertia concerns more specifically run of river generators. The potential contribution of solar or wind generation to inertia is still the subject of active research. Reserve requirements are specified in order to be symmetrically available to increase or decrease power injected into the grid.
\smallskip
\noindent
Besides the mandatory \cstr{type} attribute of any \dBlock, the group must contain all the data required by the base \code{UnitBlock}, as described in the comments to \code{UnitBlock::deserialize}( \code{netCDF::NcGroup} ). In particular, we refer to that description for the crucial \dimensions \cstr{TimeHorizon}, \cstr{NumberIntervals} and \cstr{ChangeIntervals}. The \code{netCDF::NcGroup} must then also contain:
\begin{itemize}
 \item The \variable \cstr{MinPower}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and either of size $1$ or indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals} (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then this \variable can also be indexed over \cstr{TimeHorizon}). This is meant to represent the vector \code{MinP[ t ]} that, for each time instant $t$, contains the minimum potential production value of the unit for the corresponding time step. If \cstr{MinPower} has length $1$ then \code{MinP[ t ]} contains the same value for all $t$. Otherwise, \code{MinPower[ i ]} is the fixed value of \code{MinP[ t ]} for all $t$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}. If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon}, then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{ChangeIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded. Note that it must be \code{MnP[ t ] >= 0} for all $t$.
 %
 \item The \variable \cstr{MaxPower}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and either of size $1$ or indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals} (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then this \variable can also be indexed over \cstr{TimeHorizon}). This is meant to represent the vector \code{MaxP[ t ]} that, for each time instant $t$, contains the maximum potential production value of the unit for the corresponding time step. If \cstr{MaxPower} has length $1$ then \code{MaxP[ t ]} contains the same value for all $t$. Otherwise, \code{MaxPower[ i ]} is the fixed value of \code{MaxP[ t ]} for all $t$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}. If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon}, then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{ChangeIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded. Note that it must be \code{MxP[ t ] >= MnP[ t ] [>= 0]} for all t. Yet, \code{MxP[ t ] == MnP[ t ]} is possible: it means that (at time instant $t$) the unit cannot be curtailed and cannot provide any reserve.
 %
 \item The \variable \cstr{InertiaPower}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and either of size 1 or indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals}  (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then this \variable can also be indexed over \cstr{TimeHorizon}). This is meant to represent the vector \code{IP[ t ]} which, for each time instant $t$, contains the contribution that the unit can give to the inertia constraint which depends on the active power that it is currently generating (basically, the constant to be multiplied to the active power variable) at time t for this unit. The variable is optional; if it is not defined, \code{IP[ t ] == 0} for each time instants $t$. If it has size $1$ then the entry \code{IP[ 0 ]} is assumed to contain the inertia power value for this unit and all time instants $t$. Otherwise, \code{InertiaPower[ i ]} is the fixed value of \code{IP[ t ]} for all $t$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}. If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon} then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{ChangeIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded.
%
 \item The scalar \variable \cstr{Gamma}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and not indexed over any \dimension. This \variable is used to take into account an uncertainty on the maximal potential production. Note that it must be \code{0 <= Gamma <= 1}; when \code{Gamma == 0}, the unit does not provide any reserve.
 %
 \item  The scalar \variable \cstr{Kappa}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and not indexed over any \dimension. This \variable is used to multiply to the minimum and maximum power at each time instant $t$. This is optional, if it is not provided it is taken to be \code{Kappa == 1}.
\end{itemize}

\subsubsection {\code{SlackUnitBlock}}
The \code{SlackUnitBlock} class derives from \code{UnitBlock} and implements implements a \cstr{slack} unit; a (typically, fictitious) unit capable of producing (typically, a large amount of) active power and/or primary/secondary reserve and/or inertia at any time period completely independently from each other and from all other time periods, albeit at a (typically, huge) cost. Such a unit is typically added to a Unit Commitment problem to ensure that it has a (fictitious) feasible solution, which may help solution methods. At the very least such a modified UC would produce a ``least unfeasible'' solution which can be used to identify the parts of the system that lack capacity/resources.
\smallskip
\noindent
Besides the mandatory \cstr{type} attribute of any \dBlock, the group must contain all the data required by the base \code{UnitBlock}, as described in the comments to \code{UnitBlock::deserialize}( \code{netCDF::NcGroup} ). In particular, we refer to that description for the crucial \dimensions \cstr{TimeHorizon}, \cstr{NumberIntervals} and \cstr{ChangeIntervals}. The \code{netCDF::NcGroup} must then also contain:
\begin{itemize}
 \item The \variable \cstr{MaxPower}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and either of size $1$ or indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals} (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then this \variable can also be indexed over \cstr{TimeHorizon}). This is meant to represent the vector \code{MxP[ t ]} that, for each time instant $t$, contains the maximum active power output value of the unit for the corresponding time step. If \cstr{MaxPower} has length $1$ then \code{MxP[ t ]} contains the same value for all $t$. Otherwise, \code{MaxPower[ i ]} is the fixed value of \code{MxP[ t ]} for all $t$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}. This \variable is optional, if is not provided then \code{MxP[ t ] == 0} for all $t$. Note that it must be \code{MxP[ t ] >= 0} for all $t$. If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon} then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{ChangeIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded.
 %
 \item The \variable \cstr{MaxPrimaryPower}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and either of size $1$ or indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals} (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then this \variable can also be indexed over \cstr{TimeHorizon}). This is meant to represent the vector \code{MaxPP[ t ]} that, for each time instant $t$, contains the maximum amount of primary reserve that the unit can produce in the corresponding time step. If \cstr{MaxPrimaryPower} has length $1$ then \code{MaxPP[ t ]} contains the same value for all $t$. Otherwise, \code{MaxPrimaryPower[ i ]} is the fixed value of \code{MaxPP[ t ]} for all $t$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}. This \variable is optional, if is not provided then \code{MaxPP[ t ] == 0} for all $t$. If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon} then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{ChangeIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded.
 %
 \item The \variable \cstr{MaxSecondaryPower}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and either of size $1$ or indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals} (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then this \variable can also be indexed over \cstr{TimeHorizon}). This is meant to represent the vector \code{MaxSP[ t ]} that, for each time instant $t$, contains the maximum amount of secondary reserve that the unit can produce in the corresponding time step. If \cstr{MaxSecondaryPower} has length $1$ then \code{MaxSP[ t ]} contains the same value for all $t$. Otherwise, \code{MaxSecondaryPower[ i ]} is the fixed value of \code{MaxSP[ t ]} for all $t$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}. This \variable is optional, if is not provided then \code{MaxSP[ t ] == 0} for all $t$. If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon} then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{ChangeIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded.
 %
 \item The \variable \cstr{MaxInertia}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and either of size $1$ or indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals}  (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then this \variable can also be indexed over \cstr{TimeHorizon}). This is meant to represent the vector \code{MaxI[ t ]} which, for each time instant $t$, contains the maximum ``amount of inertia'' (contribution that the SlackUnit can give to the inertia constraint) at time $t$. This \variable is optional; if it is not defined, \code{MaxI[ t ] == 0} for all time instants. If it has size $1$, then \code{MaxI[ t ] == MaxInertia[ 0 ]} for all $t$, regardless to what \cstr{NumberIntervals} says. Otherwise, \code{MaxInertia[ i ]} is the fixed value of \code{MaxI[ t ]} for all $t$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}. If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon} then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{ChangeIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded.
 %
 \item The \variable \cstr{ActivePowerCost}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and either of sie 1 or indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals}  (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then this \variable can also be indexed over \cstr{TimeHorizon}). This is meant to represent the vector \code{APC[ t ]} that, for each time instant $t$, contains the cost of producing one unit of active power at the corresponding time step. This \variable is optional, if it is not provided then it's taken to be zero(although this is a very strange setting, as it would typically imply that all the demand, or at least as much as possible of it, is satisfied by the fictitious SlackUnit rather than from ``real'' ones). If \cstr{ActivePowerCost} has length $1$ then \code{APC[ t ]} contains the same value for $t$. Otherwise, \code{ActivePowerCost[ i ]} is the fixed value of \code{APC[ t ]} for all $t$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}. If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon} then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{ChangeIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded.
 %
 \item The \variable \cstr{PrimaryCost}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and either of size 1 or indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals}  (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then this \variable can also be indexed over \cstr{TimeHorizon}). This is meant to represent the vector \code{PC[ t ]} that, for each time instant $t$, contains the cost of producing one unit of primary reserve at the corresponding time step. This \variable is optional; if it is not provided then it's taken to be zero (but this is a very strange setting, cf. the discussion in \code{ActivePowerCost}). If \cstr{PrimaryCost} has length $1$ then \code{PC[ t ]} contains the same value for $t$. Otherwise, \code{PrimaryCost[ i ]} is the fixed value of \code{PC[ t ]} for all $t$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}. If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon} then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{ChangeIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded.
 %
 \item The \variable \cstr{SecondaryCost}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and either of size 1 or indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals}  (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then this \variable can also be indexed over \cstr{TimeHorizon}). This is meant to represent the vector \code{SC[ t ]} that, for each time instant $t$, contains the cost of producing one unit of secondary reserve at the corresponding time step. This \variable is optional; if it is not provided then it's taken to be zero (but this is a very strange setting, cf. the discussion in \code{ActivePowerCost}). If \cstr{SecondaryCost} has length $1$ then \code{SC[ t ]} contains the same value for $t$. Otherwise, \code{SecondaryCost[ i ]} is the fixed value of \code{SC[ t ]} for all $t$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}. If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon} then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{ChangeIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded.
 %
 \item The \variable \cstr{InertiaCost}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and either of size 1 or indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals}  (if \cstr{NumberIntervals} is not provided, then this \variable can also be indexed over \cstr{TimeHorizon}). This is meant to represent the vector \code{IC[ t ]} that, for each time instant $t$, contains the cost of producing ``one unit'' of inertia. Since the inertia-producing variable is \code{u[ t ]}, which is in the interval \code{[ 0 , 1 ]}, the cost of \code{u[ t ]} is \code{MaxI[ t ] * IC[ t ]}; in other words, \code{u[ t ]} represents the fraction of the maximum possible amount of inertia (\code{MaxI[ t ]}) that can be produced at time step $t$. This \variable is optional; if it is not provided then it's taken to be zero(but this is a very strange setting, cf. the discussion in \code{ActivePowerCost}). If \cstr{InertiaCost} has length $1$ then \code{IC[ t ]} contains the same value for $t$. Otherwise, \code{InertiaCost[ i ]} is the fixed value of \code{IC[ t ]} for all $t$ in the interval \code{[ ChangeIntervals[ i - 1 ] , ChangeIntervals[ i ] ]}, with the assumption that \code{ChangeIntervals[ - 1 ] = 0}. If \code{NumberIntervals <= 1} or \code{NumberIntervals >= TimeHorizon} then the mapping clearly does not require \cstr{ChangeIntervals}, which in fact is not loaded.
\end{itemize}

%- - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - -
\subsection {\code{HydroSystemUnitBlock}}\label{sec:HydroSystemUnitBlock}
The class \code{HydroSystemUnitBlock}, which derives from the \Block, in order to define a base class for any possible \cstr{hydro unit} plus the linking \code{PolyhedralFunctionBlock} to describe the future value of water function in a \code{UCBlock}. Besides the mandatory \cstr{type} attribute of any \dBlock, the group should contain the following:
\begin{itemize}
\item The \dimension \cstr{NumberHydroUnits} containing the number of hydro units (\code{HydroUnitBlock}) in the problem.
%
\item The \groups \cstr{HydroUnitBlock\_0}, \cstr{HydroUnitBlock\_1}, \dots, \cstr{HydroUnitBlock\_(n-1)}, with \code{n == NumberHydroUnits}, containing each one \code{HydroUnitBlock}.
%
\item The \group \cstr{PolyhedralFunctionBlock} which contains a \code{PolyhedralFunctionBlock}, whose \code{PolyhedralFunction} represents the future value of the water (a.k.a. \cstr{Bellman values}) left at the end of the time horizon in all the reservoirs of all the \code{HydroUnitBlock} of the \code{HydroSystemUnitBlock}.
%
\end{itemize}
The future value of water function is represented by the single \code{PolyhedralFunction} which lives inside the \code{PolyhedralFunctionBlock}. The vector of \cstr{active} variable of \code{PolyhedralFunction} is therefore in a one-to-one correspondence with the set of \code{ColVariable} in the \code{HydroUnitBlock} that represent the amount of water left in each reservoir at the end of the time horizon. Thus, it is necessary to specify the order of the active \code{ColVariable} of the \code{PolyhedralFunction}. Let us denote by \code{X[ 0 ], X[ 1 ], \dots, X[ R - 1 ]} the vector of active \code{ColVariable} (i.e., \code{X[ i ]} is the one returned by \code{get\_active\_var( i )} and \code{R = get\_num\_active\_var())}. Since each \code{HydroUnitBlock} can have more than one reservoir (cf. \code{HydroUnitBlock::get\_number\_reservoirs())}, $R$ is just the total number of reservoir, which is computed by just calling \code{get\_number\_reservoirs()} on each of the \code{HydroUnitBlock} and summing all the results. Clearly, \code{R >= NumberHydroUnits}. Some of the \code{HydroUnitBlock} may have just one reservoir; if this happens for all the hydro unit blocks (but this is not likely), then \code{R == NumberHydroUnits}. In this case the mapping is obvious: \code{X[ i ]} is the \code{ColVariable} that represent the amount of water left in the only reservoir of \code{HydroUnitBlock\_i} at the end of the time horizon. When, instead, \code{R > NumberHydroUnits}, a mapping must be defined. The mapping is the obvious one: \code{HydroUnitBlock} have an ordering \code{n = 0, 1, \dots, NumberHydroUnits - 1}  (cf. the \groups \cstr{HydroUnitBlock\_0}, \cstr{HydroUnitBlock\_1}, \dots above), and the reservoirs into each \code{HydroUnitBlock} also have a natural ordering. Thus, in general the mapping is:
\begin{itemize}
\item \code{X[ 0 ] =} \code{ColVariable} representing the amount of water left in the first reservoir of \code{HydroUnitBlock\_0} at the end of the time horizon
%
\item \code{X[ 1 ] =} \code{ColVariable} representing the amount of water left in the second reservoir of \code{HydroUnitBlock\_0} at the end of the time horizon
%
\item \code{\dots}
%
\item \code{X[ k ] =} \code{ColVariable} representing the amount of water left in the $k$-th reservoir of \code{HydroUnitBlock\_0} at the end of the time horizon, with \code{k = HydroUnitBlock\_0->get\_number\_reservoirs()}
%
\item \code{X[ k + 1 ] =} \code{ColVariable} representing the amount of water left in the first reservoir of \code{HydroUnitBlock\_1} at the end of the time horizon
%
\item \code{X[ k + 2 ] =} \code{ColVariable} representing the amount of water left in the second reservoir of \code{HydroUnitBlock\_1} at the end of the time horizon
%
\item \code{\dots}
\end{itemize}
This must be the format of the data (linear inequalities) that define the \code{PolyhedralFunction}: the $i$-th entry of each vector is related to the future value of the water stored in the reservoir identified by the above mapping. See \code{PolyhedralFunction::deserialize()} for details about how the data must be stored in the \code{PolyhedralFunctionBlock} \group.

%- - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - -
\subsection {\code{PolyhedralFunctionBlock}}\label{sec:PolyhedralFunctionBlock}
The class \code{PolyhedralFunctionBlock} derives from \code{AbstractBlock} to define the following concept: a \Block whose sole distinguishing feature is a \code{PolyhedralFunction} as objective, but which can otherwise contain any kind of ``abstract'' Variable and Constraint (provided these are handled by the base \code{AbstractBlock} class). The rationale for the \code{PolyhedralFunctionBlock} class is that a \code{PolyhedralFunction} is a perfectly fine object in itself, but one that several solvers cannot easily deal with in its ``natural'' form. However, a \code{PolyhedralFunction} can also be represented in a very natural way by means of one extra continuous \code{ColVariable} plus a finite set of (dynamic) \code{FRowConstraint} with \code{LinearFunction} inside (a.k.a., linear constraints). Thus, the main feature that \code{PolyhedralFunctionBlock} implements is the ability to ``present'' itself (construct an ``abstract representation'') as either having a \code{FRealObjective} with a \code{PolyhedralFunction} inside, or having a set of (continuous) \code{ColVariable} and (linear) \code{RowConstraint}. We call the first the ``natural representation''  of a \code{PolyhedralFunctionBlock}, and the latter its ``linearized representation''. Note that most individual changes in the \code{PolyhedralFunction} result in many changes to the ``linearized representation'', that are properly bunched into appropriate \code{GroupModification}. Conversely, many individual changes to the ``linearized representation'' cannot (or would be too complex to) be implemented in the \code{PolyhedralFunction}, because each one of them individually would lead it to end in a partly inconsistent state, and only a co-ordinated set of them (say, properly bunched into an appropriate \code{GroupModification}) would work. Hence, a number of changes to the ``linearized representation'' are not allowed; see the comments to the protected method \code{guts\_of\_add\_Modification\_LR()} for details. Other than that, \code{PolyhedralFunctionBlock} entirely relies on the machinery provided by \code{AbstractBlock} to handle all the rest of the \Block, and therefore is subject to the limitations of that class regarding what kind of \code{Constraint}, \code{Variable} and \code{Objective} are supported. Besides the mandatory \cstr{type} attribute of any \dBlock, the group should contain the following:
\begin{itemize}
\item all the data necessary to describe a \code{PolyhedralFunction}; see \S \ref{sec:PolyhedralFunction} for details;
%
\item any other data necessary to represent the ``arbitrary'' part of the
  \code{AbstractBlock}; see \code{AbstractBlock::\-de\-se\-rialize()} for details.
\end{itemize}


\subsubsection {\code{PolyhedralFunction}} \label{sec:PolyhedralFunction}
The \code{PolyhedralFunction} class derives from \code{C05Function}, and defines a simple implementation of a convex (or concave) \code{Function} defined by the maximum (or minimum) of a ``small'' number of explicitly provided affine forms. In other words, if the \code{PolyhedralFunction} depends on a set of $n$ \code{ColVariable}, its input data is a $m \times n$ matrix $A$ and a $m \times 1$ vector $b$ (with $m$ given and ``small''). Serialize a \code{PolyhedralFunction} into a \code{netCDF::NcGroup}, with the following format:

\begin{itemize}
\item The \dimension \cstr{PolyFunction\_NumVar} containing the number of columns of the $A$ matrix, i.e., the number of active variables.
%
\item The \dimension \cstr{PolyFunction\_NumRow} containing the number of rows of the $A$ matrix. The dimension is optional, if it is not provided than $0$ (no rows) is assumed.
%
\item The \variable \cstr{PolyFunction\_A}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and indexed over both the \dimensions \code{NumRow} and \code{NumVar} (in this order); it contains the(row-major) representation of the matrix $A$. The variable is only optional if \code{NumRow == 0}.
%
\item The \variable \cstr{PolyFunction\_b}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and indexed over the \dimension \code{NumRow}, which contains the vector $b$. The variable is only optional if \code{NumRow == 0}.
%
\item The \dimension \cstr{PolyFunction\_sign} (actually a bool), which contains the ``verse'' of the \code{PolyhedralFunction}, i.e., true for a convex max-function and false for a concave min-function (encoded in the obvious way, i.e., zero for false, nonzero for true). The \variable is optional, if it is not provided true is assumed.
%
\item The scalar \variable \cstr{PolyFunction\_lb}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and not indexed over any \dimension, which contains the global lower (if \code{PolyFunction\_sign ==} true, upper otherwise) bound on the value of the function over all the space. The \variable is optional; if it is not provided it means that no finite lower (upper) bound exists, i.e., the lower (upper) bound is \code{-inf} (\code{+inf}).
\end{itemize}

%- - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - -
\subsection {\code{NetworkBlock}}

The class \code{NetworkBlock}, which derives from the \Block, defines the basic interface for the constraints/optimization problems which describe the behaviour of the network in a specific time instant that can optionally span a number of sub time instants, in the Unit Commitment (UC) problem, as represented in \code{UCBlock}. The base class handles only basic information: it allows to read/set the characteristic of the network, and the active power demand at the different nodes in the given time instant or for the all the sub time intants spanned. This information is actually bunched together in a small ``passive'' \code{NetworkData} object (no methods, just a data repository) that can be either de-serialized or passed ready-made (typically, by the \code{UCBlock}). Details of the kind of network that is implemented (EC, DC equations, AC equations, OPF, \dots) are entirely demanded to derived objects. The interface between a \code{NetworkBlock} and the rest of the UC is just the matrix of node per sub time instants injection variables, which will have to satisfy the technical constraints of the network. Besides the mandatory \cstr{type} attribute of any \dBlock, the group should contain the following:
\begin{itemize}
 \item Optionally, the \dimensions and variables necessary to a \code{NetworkData} object, that describe the network. All that is optional, because the \code{NetworkData} object can alternatively be passed to the \code{NetworkBlock} via a call to \code{set\_NetworkData()}. Note that if \code{set\_NetworkData()} is called, but the representation of a \code{NetworkData} object is found in the NcGroup, then the \code{NetworkData} passed by \code{set\_NetworkData()} is ignored, and a new \code{NetworkData} object is read from the \code{NcGroup} and used instead.
 %
 \item The \cstr{ActiveDemand}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble}, and of size \cstr{NumberNodes}. If the \code{NetworkData} object description is present in the \code{NcGroup} this is the \dimension \cstr{NumberNodes}, but the \code{NetworkData} object is optional and it may not be there. Thus if \cstr{NumberNodes} is not there and \cstr{ActiveDemand} is, then the \code{NetworkData} object must have been passed by \code{set\_NetworkData()}, and the number of nodes can be read via \code{NetworkBlock::NetworkData::get\_number\_nodes()}. However, \cstr{ActiveDemand} itself is optional. If it is not found in the \code{NcGroup}, then it \highlight{must} be passed (either before or after the call to \code{deserialize()}) by calling \code{set\_ActiveDemand()}. Since both groups of data are optional, the \code{NcGroup}  can actually be empty which implies that all the data will be (or have been) passed by the in-memory interface. In this case, it would clearly be preferable to \highlight{entirely avoid the \code{NcGroup} to be there}, and in fact \code{UCBlock} has provisions for the \code{NcGroup} describing the \code{NetworkBlock} to be optional (see \code{UCBlock} in \S \ref{sec:UCBlock}).
\end{itemize}

\subsubsection {\code{DCNetworkBlock}}
The \code{DCNetworkBlock} class derives from \code{NetworkBlock}, and defines the standard linear constraints corresponding to the ``DC model'' of the transmission network in the Unit Commitment problem.

\subsubsection  {\code{DCNetworkData}} \label{sec:DCNetworkData}
The \code{DCNetworkData} class is a nested sub-class which only serves to have a quick way to load all the basic data (topology and electrical characteristics) that describe the transmission network. The rationale is that while often the network does not change during the (short) time horizon of UC, it makes sense to allow for this to happen. This means that individual \code{NetworkBlock} objects may in principle have different \code{DCNetworkData}, but most often they can share the same. By bunching all the information together we make it easy for this sharing to happen.
\smallskip
\noindent
Deserialize a \code{DCNetworkData} out of a \code{netCDF::NcGroup}, which should contain the following:
\begin{itemize}
 \item the \dimension \cstr{NumberNodes} containing the number of nodes in the problem; this \dimension is optional, if it is not provided then it is taken to be \code{== 1};
 %
 \smallskip
 \noindent
 If \code{NumberNodes == 1} (equivalently, it is not provided), the network is a ``bus'' formed of only one node, and therefore all the subsequent information need not to be present since it is not loaded. If \code{NumberNodes > 1}, then all the subsequent information is mandatory:
 %
 \item the \dimension \cstr{NumberLines} containing the number of lines in the transmission network;
 %
 \item the \variable \cstr{StartLine}, of type \code{netCDF::NcUint} and indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberLines}; the $i$-th entry of the variable is the starting point of the line (a number in \code{0, \dots, NumberLines - 1}). Note that lines are not oriented, but the flow of energy is; that is, a positive flow along line $i$ means that energy is being taken away from \code{StartLine[ i ]} and delivered to \code{EndLine[ i ]} (see next), a negative flow means vice-versa. Note that lines names here go from $0$ to \code{NLines.getSize() - 1};
 %
 \item the \variable \cstr{EndLine}, of type \code{netCDF::NcUint} and indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberLines}; the $i$-th entry of the variable is the ending point of the line (a number in \code{0, \dots, NumberLines - 1}. Note that lines are not oriented, but see above). \code{StartLine[ i ] == EndLine[ i ]} (a self-loop) is not allowed, but multiple lines between the same pair of nodes are. Note that lines names here go from $0$ to \code{NLines.getSize() - 1};
 %
 \item the \variable \cstr{MinPowerFlow}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberLines}; the $i$-th entry of the variable is assumed to contain the minimum power flow on line $i$ (note that this is typically a negative number as lines are bi-directional, see above);
%
 \item the \variable \cstr{MaxPowerFlow}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberLines}; the $i$-th entry of the variable is assumed to contain the maximum power flow at line $i$ (a non-negative number);
 %
 \item the \variable \cstr{Susceptance}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and indexed over the \dimension \cstr{NumberLines}; the $i$-th entry of this variable is assumed to contain the susceptance of line $i$. Note that this is strictly a positive value.
\end{itemize}
%

\subsubsection  {\code{ECNetworkBlock}}
The \code{ECNetworkBlock} class, which derives from \code{NetworkBlock} and describe the behaviour of the energy community network at a specific time instant or in a time interval, i.e., a peak period that can span an arbitrary number of sub time horizons, in the Unit Commitment problem.

\subsubsection  {\code{ECNetworkData}} \label{sec:ECNetworkData}
The \code{ECNetworkData} class is a nested sub-class which only serves to have a quick way to load all the basic data that describe the community network. The rationale is that while often the network does not change during the (short) time horizon of UC, it makes sense to allow for this to happen. This means that individual \code{NetworkBlock} objects may in principle have different \code{ECNetworkData}, but most often they can share the same. By bunching all the information together we make it easy for this sharing to happen.
\smallskip
\noindent
Deserialize an \code{ECNetworkData} out of a \code{netCDF::NcGroup}, which should contain the following:
\begin{itemize}
  \item the \dimension \cstr{NumberNodes} containing the number of nodes in the problem; this \dimension is mandatory, it cannot be \code{== 1} since it cannot exist an energy community with just one user;
 %
 \item the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals} containing the number of intervals spanned by this current time horizon to which \code{ECNetworkBlock} refers;
 %
 \item the \variable \cstr{BuyPrice}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and containing the tariff that the user pays to buy electricity for all the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals} spanned by the current time horizon;
 %
 \item the \variable \cstr{SellPrice}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and containing the ariff that the user gains to sell electricity for all the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals} spanned by the current time horizon;
 %
 \item the \variable \cstr{RewardPrice}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and containing the tariff that the user gains when it absorbs power from the microgrid instead of from the public grid for all the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals} spanned by the current time horizon;
 %
 \item the \variable \cstr{PeakTariff}, of type \code{netCDF::NcDouble} and containing the tariff that the user gains when it absorbs power from the microgrid instead of from the public grid for all the \dimension \cstr{NumberIntervals} spanned by the current time horizon.
\end{itemize}
%
%- - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - -
%\subsection {\code{HeatBlock}}
%
%The \code{HeatBlock} class implements the \Block concept [see \code{Block.h}] for a set of ``heat units'' as required in the plan4res project. The constraints regarding heat management in the plan4res project can be described separately for each \code{HeatBlock}. In the parlance of plan4res, a \code{HeatBlock} is the intersection of an Energy Cell and a heat-ID: a set of heat-producing units that are geographically close enough to exchange heat of the same type, together with possibly a (single) storage for this type of heat, in order to satisfy a given heat demand. \code{HeatBlock}s are only connected to each other because some heat-producing units are also electricity-producing ones, where heat is a by-product or a co-product of electricity. However, this linking ``happens at the level of the main UC model'', and therefore it is not required for the description of the internal constraints of a \code{HeatBlock}. All this on a given time horizon, as in the UC problem. The operations of the heat generating unit are described on a discrete time horizon where there may exist some heat-producing units, possibly of a single heat storage, and of the demand that has to be satisfied.

The \dimension is optional because the same information may be passed via the method \code{set\_time\_horizon()}, or directly retrieved from the father if it is a \code{UCBlock}; see the comments to \code{set\_time\_horizon()} for details;

Individual data can also have specific provisions for the case where the data is all equal despite \cstr{NumberIntervals} saying differently.
